- 2026-10-16: Started D-2/D-22 with Wasmoon 1.16.0 pinned exactly (package LICENSE: MIT, copyright Gabriel Francisco; its @types/emscripten dependency is MIT). Runtime construction requires an explicit asset location; the browser adapter uses Vite asset emission rather than the default CDN. Each calculation can own an isolated Lua VM, retain globals across synchronous hooks and dispose it idempotently. Named chunks preserve source line numbers without generated wrapper lines; execution errors close the potentially partially modified VM. Successful calls restore the Lua stack. Unit checks cover isolation, repeated execution/stack cleanup, synchronous bindings, disposal and compile/runtime line 3 errors. A built Chromium worker loads the same-origin WASM and verifies execution, diagnostics and replacement-session isolation; this check is included in Pages CI. This is runtime infrastructure only: legacy arrays/functions, project transactionality, worker calculation integration and AC-7/7a/8 acceptance remain open. Existing real-engine script guards stay in place and the deployed app remains mock.

- 2026-10-16: Traced script.cpp initialization and scriptmetaarray.cpp before adding the scalar script-array workspace in model/. The 16 scalar names retain their numeric type IDs, substrate-first zero-based indices and maximum-index size convention (empty is -1). Layer optics/directions, Gaussian profile fields, script parameters and element f1/f2 map without unit scaling; signed temporary values are not clamped, and nonfinite writes fail. The workspace clones input and returns independent snapshots, preparing transactional execution without publishing partial script changes. Element-mode layer arrays explicitly reject access until the active film is prepared; they must not accidentally operate on compound storage. Tensor access also sets the original active layer anisotropic even on reads, and solution-array bounds depend on current qz length; both require subsequent calculation-state bindings. This step does not expose arrays in Lua or establish project-level AC-8; VM userdata bindings, all functions/globals and real-engine integration remain open.

- 2026-10-17: Exposed the scalar workspace through a Wasmoon type extension using actual LuaBook.array userdata and array.new/get/set/size. Aliases share underlying state; declared maximum indices and backing storage are both checked, avoiding legacy undefined access if users construct oversized aliases. Reads explicitly use lua_pushnumber, preserving double arithmetic and negative zero instead of Wasmoon default integral-value promotion. Numeric strings use Lua conversion. Binding failures include the caller source location and close the session through the existing execution path; GC releases host references. Node and Chromium checks exercise bracket/API calls, aliases, Lua type/math.type, signed zero, source immutability, invalid arguments and line-3 diagnostics. Inspection of the bundled Lua 5.1 luaconf.h found compiler-dependent fractional integer conversion (Pentium rounding trick versus C truncation); fractional indices/type IDs/sizes therefore fail explicitly pending original-build verification rather than selecting an unverified policy. This remains partial script support: tensor/result arrays, element objects, remaining globals/functions, hooks and project-level acceptance are not complete.

- 2026-10-17: Added the calculation-owned solution workspace for solution_qz/R/Rp/Rm/energy (legacy IDs 6–10). Userdata holds a column accessor rather than a stale result array, so references and aliases survive atomic result replacement. array.size preserves the declared -1; getelem-style bounds then use the current qz length for any handle with that size, including scalar aliases. Explicit alias sizes retain fixed bounds. Backing-column checks reject missing/short columns and oversized aliases rather than reproducing undefined reads. Values are writable doubles without scaling; replacement inputs and returned snapshots are independent and nonfinite replacements leave previous data intact. Node and Chromium worker checks cover replacement, aliases, dynamic/fixed bounds, missing columns, writes and source isolation. This supplies result storage only; Lua calculation functions still need to populate it, and tensor arrays, element objects, globals/hooks and project-level acceptance remain open.

- 2026-10-17: Added an isolated active-film tensor workspace and exposed all 18 legacy real/imaginary arrays through the existing userdata binding. IDs 15–23 and 25–33 follow row-major complex components; reserved ID 24 remains unsupported because the original initializes no corresponding global and its accessor falls through to NULL. Both reads and writes set the accessed layer anisotropic, matching getelem. Refresh replaces tensors atomically at a fixed layer count while existing aliases remain live; snapshots and matrix overrides are independent copies. Native tensor construction remains injected upstream, not duplicated in Lua/TypeScript. - 2026-10-17: Traced scriptmetaobject.cpp and added real ElementObject userdata, its method metatable/global element, named element globals and constructor aliases. Invalid backing indices and nonfinite writes fail explicitly. Legacy get/set are not slab operations: they access one function-static double initially 3.14 shared across elements and Lua states; that scratch behavior is preserved in the worker module, without leaking project state. setf validates its arguments then reports native table lookup not supported yet pending integration. - 2026-10-17: Connected compound FillTensors and SetDeltaBeta to injected native construction/table lookup. Traced LuaSetDeltaBeta, CMaterialConstants::Getdb, CDeltaBeta and CMATRIX constructors: SetDeltaBeta copies scalar/magnetic constants and imported .e values, or resets the tensor to zero/isotropic without .e; it does not implicitly FillTensors. The resolved per-layer table is cached, so subsequent SetName does not silently replace it. Missing/empty/out-of-range primary tables retain state. - 2026-10-17: Traced GUI LuaPrintMessage and added an explicit print/write message sink. Both globals use the same function, concatenate arguments without separators and emit one entry per invocation, including empty calls; non-string/non-number arguments contribute empty text as the legacy lua_tostring/AnsiString path does. C-string termination is retained per argument. Numeric values use Lua string.format with %.14g, avoiding Lua 5.4 tostring adding .0 to integral doubles. Formatting library functions are captured during installation; sink failures retain the user call line and dispose the session. - 2026-10-17: Added compile/runHook to the calculation-owned Lua session, following CScript CompileScript/StartScript. A compiled chunk serves as Start without inserted source lines; execution resolves the global Start on every call, preserving script replacement of the entry function. Each call supplies energy, epsilon, resolution, energyscan_qz and renewocs as doubles, retaining other Lua globals while function locals are fresh. Successful output reads the four numeric globals (including numeric strings) and returns renewOpticalConstants=0; script writes to renewocs are not read back, matching legacy. Invalid numeric outputs and compile/runtime errors dispose the session and leave the caller state unchanged. Unit tests cover compilation without execution, repeat calls, Start replacement, double/negative-zero values, flag consumption and line-3 failures. Chromium combines repeated hooks with the bound scalar arrays. These are explicit call-state values: project energy/wavelength conversion, pre-call flag consumption within nested calculations, automatic optical refresh, scan/fit dispatch and UI execution still need orchestration; public engine selection stays mock.

- 2026-10-17: Added isolated script-workspace refresh and runProjectHook. Refresh copies new project values while existing scalar array accessors and element userdata resolve current storage; layer/profile/parameter/element/slab counts, mode and element names must remain stable or a new session is required. A private wavelength tracks Variablen exactly between calls using hc=12398.326; external refresh converts input energy once, hook entry converts wavelength to energy, and successful readback stores wavelength before publishing project energy. renewocs is consumed before executing, making nested entry observe zero, and script writes to that global remain ignored. Only successful execution/readback yields a project snapshot; failures close the session and caller projects remain unchanged. Unit tests cover persistent Lua globals, refreshed factor/slab aliases, conversion order, output mapping, structural rejection and invalid-energy disposal. Chromium now exercises repeated compiled hooks through this project adapter. Automatic table/scan/fit orchestration, callbacks for nested calculations and full script acceptance remain open.

- 2026-10-17: Connected BeforeCalculation to standalone compound qz requests in the explicit real worker after tracing CalcReflectivityFilm/CalcAsymmetrie: automatic table refresh precedes the script, which precedes layer packing. - 2026-10-17: Generalized the partial refresh helper and reconciled its caller policies. Dataset-driven CalcEScan/CalcDicEScan skip a point when Filldb returns false, preserving updates to valid layers and cursor history; the redundant all-layer preflight is removed from the numerical energy loop, and missing/empty primaries now follow that path. - 2026-10-17: Made createBeforeCalculation reusable across caller-supplied project/optical states: bindings and compiled Start are installed once, user globals and resolved script table caches persist, subsequent project/tensor values refresh behind existing handles, and cursor maps are copied on input/output to keep earlier snapshots independent. Changed script source requires a new session; preparation/execution failures close it. Automatic refresh must therefore not preseed the script cache; SetName before the first SetDeltaBeta uses the new name, while subsequent calls keep the first successfully resolved table. - 2026-10-17: Traced ImplementFitVariable and CMultiFit hook placement before enabling repeated calculation hooks. Scalar candidate updates do not call FillTensors, so script-visible isotropic tensor values must survive even though the matrix solver can derive isotropic optics from scalar fields. Extended serial optical state with optional complete tensor snapshots, alongside existing anisotropic overrides. BeforeCalculation uses retained snapshots when supplied and returns independent complete state; optical refresh reports updated layers so qz, energy and fit initialization invalidate only those entries, retaining missing/out-of-range layer state. Tests cover reading an old isotropic tensor after scalar edits, explicit FillTensors replacement, read-induced anisotropy, selective refresh and source snapshot isolation. Workspace refresh also retains its private wavelength when energy is unchanged, avoiding an unnecessary extra round trip. Legacy CMultiFit has a constructor hook plus an objective-level renewocs=1 hook before dataset calls; that timing remains to be wired, as do energy hooks. No fitting-script acceptance is claimed.


- 2026-10-18: Connected BeforeCalculation to real compound qz fitting. Rechecked the four-argument CScript constructor (executes once without setting renewocs), CStateHandler default renewocs=0, CMultiFit::Error (candidate mapping, optional optical refresh, renewocs=1 hook) and serial CalcErrorOfDataset calls. The fit owns one Lua session; initialization runs once, each objective explicitly renews, and each qz calculation consumes the cleared flag. Script-updated project settings now accompany curve state for serial dataset/evaluation propagation, while temporary packing flags do not disable later automatic refresh or scripting. Complete tensor state and table cursors continue across calls; best snapshots retain their evaluated state. FitResult carries captured print/write messages; UI log routing remains pending. Node tests exercise two objectives and two datasets, persistent epsilon/layer writes, best-state isolation and an early explicit energy-fit guard. - 2026-10-19: Prepared LuaSession for nested calculation callbacks by fixing its disposal lifecycle. - 2026-10-19: Corrected the rationale for the earlier element-mode Lua active-film guard. GetSchichtEingabe updates el slabs in element mode and leaves mfs stored separately; GetState copies both mfs/el and calls dtoz on stored mfs; scriptmetaarray accesses film->mfs without a mode branch; SetName/SetChemicalFormula/SetDeltaBeta likewise target stored mfs (SetDeltaBeta explicitly selects MATERIAL tables). FillLayers sends Element2Material output to a separate mfsdest, not back into the stored film. Therefore generated element calculation layers are not prerequisites for those Lua arrays/functions. Removed the conservative unsupported-mode guard, preserving separate stored-layer and element-slab semantics; this supersedes the guard rationale in the earlier preparation notes, without authorizing a legacy semantic deviation.

- 2026-10-19: Connected element scattering-factor and slab-optical spectra to persistent BeforeCalculation sessions. Initial automatic state refreshes ff/ffm and tensors, while manual state constructs tensors; stored layer tensor state remains separate. Every nonzero factor-grid point (every positive optical-grid point) refreshes partial element state then runs the hook, retaining Lua globals and temporary nested-session context. Output coordinates and optical-spectrum denominators use the captured table energy even if Lua changes energy. Live slab/factor edits, FillTensors and stored-layer optics use the shared workspace; messages stream and retain fallback transcripts with cancellation/error/disposal guards. Tests cover independent layer/slab storage, constructor/per-point counts, both automatic settings, range retention, zero-grid behavior, source-line errors and input isolation. Chromium uses real interpolation/tensors to compare both spectrum types and output delivery. This completes initial scripted imported-table spectra, not full element reflectivity, production erf selection or nested calculation callbacks. All 359 ordinary app tests pass (four environment-dependent checks skipped); lint, production build and the real-service Chromium harness pass.

- 2026-10-19: Source review of CMultiFit::Error found that its active supported-target Q term adds Q * (activevars[i] - state.fitvariables[i].var) * (activevars[i] - state.fitvariables[i].var) after weighted datasets, skipping candidates whose absolute value is strictly below 1e-6. The metadata constructor initially fills activevars from starting values, but that vector is mutable optimizer state; it must not be silently treated as a fixed prior in the new optimizer. Added a pure prepared-vector arithmetic component with explicit reference/candidate inputs, preserved multiplication/accumulation order and finite-result validation. All 430 ordinary app tests pass (four environment-dependent checks skipped), along with lint and production build. This helper has analytical unit coverage only; no original-optimizer or browser Q-fit acceptance is claimed.

- 2026-10-19: Traced the magnetic matrix whole-project divergence to one-ULP platform differences in complex-root construction, atan2, cos and asin, amplified by nearly coincident original quartic roots and normalized eigenvectors. Identical original-loaded tensors match in native C++; passing native incident angles also eliminated the last isolated WASM mismatches. Preserved the original polynomial, guarded Newton passes, mode ordering and tolerance. The WASM matrix path now uses pinned, unmodified glibc atan2/cos/asin sources from revision 04e750e75b73957cf1c791535a3f4319534a52fc plus C complex primitives, avoiding libc++ polar square roots. The public upstream components are LGPL-2.1-or-later, compatible with GPL-3.0 under D-31; upstream notices/identifiers are retained as third-party library code, complete sources and build shims are in engine/third_party/glibc_math, and the licence/notices ship with the static app and are linked from Help → License. No Numerical Recipes or private legacy source is included. Source hashes are checked at WASM configuration and in native CI; the precision gate now includes vendored source/tables and distinguishes header filenames from numeric types. Added 4,800 magnetic public-stack intensity comparisons covering both beams and exact/fast angular convolution to Node/Chromium tests. This resolves the observed 24-case discrepancy without a D-29 exception; original quartic residual limitations and full corpus/other-platform acceptance remain open. Final validation: all 24 complete-project Chromium variants pass (14,400 values, zero D-23 mismatches; maximum positive-pair log10 error about 1.11138e-11). All 23 native tests pass with private-original comparisons enabled; expanded Node/Chromium comparisons pass, including 18,144 matrix intensities. All 431 app tests pass (five environment-dependent checks skipped), lint and production build pass. A deliberate temporary upstream-header mutation is rejected by the CMake hash gate; restoring the pinned bytes restores a successful build. The full real-service Chromium harness also passes, including matrix fits/χ², scripts/tables and blocked-fit Stop (485 ms).

- 2026-10-19: Found float intermediates in the original LayerSegmentation.cpp error-function helpers: the double-interface series uses float accumulation variables, and the continued fraction also keeps float state. No Numerical Recipes implementation or generated lookup table is copied into public code. Against 20,001 original direct-function samples, 9,112 exceed unchanged D-23; maximum absolute difference is 2.4898593131705837e-7. This diagnostic is not a production replacement. - 2026-10-19: Implemented a native double-only Fourier postprocessing component after tracing CDataSet::FFT. It preserves the extra qz^4 multiplication after earlier postprocessing, the legacy 3.141592 constant and N+1 window denominator, unnormalized forward transform magnitude, floor(N/2) truncation, zero DC and depth coordinates based on the last qz value. The original interpolated R0 buffer is never passed to FFTW, so the component intentionally transforms the original point order, including unordered/constant grids. Independently implemented radix-two FFT plus chirp convolution for arbitrary sizes; padding belongs to the convolution and does not change the requested DFT length. Chirp index squares use 64-bit integers to remain valid on WASM's 32-bit size_t. The C API accepts 2..1,000,000 points, stages outputs atomically and explicitly rejects invalid/nonfinite inputs or results. A hash-gated private original-method comparison using system FFTW matches 10,122 values across 48 synthetic cases at unchanged D-23; independent direct DFT, zero/impulse/two-impulse interference, large non-power-of-two length and failure checks pass. All 24 native CTest groups (including the double-only gate) and 435 app tests, lint and production build pass. No NR code or new dependency is introduced. WASM adapter, worker/output-column semantics and full Fourier acceptance remain open; existing real-engine rejection remains until integration is verified.

- 2026-10-20: Added a native clean-room simplex-annealing primitive under D-32 using Cardoso, Salcedo and de Azevedo (1996), equations 9-10 (https://doi.org/10.1016/0098-1354(95)00221-9), read from the authors' university-hosted PDF. No NR optimizer code was consulted or translated. The existing independent Nelder-Mead moves now optionally compare upward-perturbed vertex scores and downward-perturbed candidate scores, retaining true objective values for best/progress. The separate C API accepts temperature, additive step per completed simplex iteration, and a seed; temperature clamps to zero, convergence is allowed only at zero, and hard work limits govern constant/heating schedules. mt19937 draws map explicitly to (0,1); this does not claim legacy RNG trajectories or paper-specific adaptive cooling/constraint schemes. UI labels/defaults were checked in FitAlgorithmFrame.dfm, but project-level schedule mapping is not enabled yet. Tests prove exact zero-temperature equivalence, deterministic replay, quadratic recovery, cancellation/budgets and atomic failures (including thermal overflow). - 2026-10-22: Extended the existing private Q ownership probe with per-invocation/callback observations. - 2026-10-22: Added an optional reference lifecycle API to the independent ordinary Nelder-Mead implementation. - 2026-10-23: Integrate dimension-adaptive ordinary Nelder-Mead from Gao and Han (2012), equation 4.1 (https://doi.org/10.1007/s10589-010-9329-3), under the clean-room optimizer policy D-32. A separate C API/WASM function retains the existing classical API and annealing behavior; ordinary real-worker fits select the adaptive function. Budgets, bound application, Q-reference lifecycle and stopping tolerances are unchanged. Synthetic dimension studies show substantial improvements on several larger problems but a worse local basin for ten-dimensional Rosenbrock, so no universal improvement or global-minimum guarantee is claimed. D-40: [accepted] **Prioritize complete usable workflows; defer broad numerical acceptance to final review** (2026-10-24)
- The user requests that the program work completely first and prefers final manual accuracy checks using real examples. Do not continue broad corpus sweeps, optimizer-quality investigations or precision tuning as the default next task while normal workflows remain unavailable.
- During implementation, use targeted checks for the affected behavior, worker cancellation, data preservation and clear errors. Reuse unchanged passing results and run expensive checks sequentially, as requested in the updated AGENTS.md testing rules. Existing mandatory pre-commit checks remain.
- The discussed provisional review criteria are absolute log10-reflectivity difference <= 0.001 for positive values and absolute normalized-asymmetry difference <= 0.001. These are program-comparison criteria, not claims about measurement uncertainty. Treatment of zero/very low reflectivity and final representative examples remains to be agreed during final review; 1e-8 is not an agreed cutoff.
- Existing D-23/D-28 regression tests are not silently loosened or removed. Functional completion and final numerical acceptance remain distinct; passing a narrow check cannot close full acceptance. This changes work priority, not legacy physics, Lua semantics, licensing or the unresolved error-function rounding choice. That choice has been put to the user with the recommendation to use a full-double standard error function so the blocked production workflows can be enabled.

D-41: [accepted] **Use full double precision for production error-function generation; do not emulate legacy float rounding** (2026-10-24)
- The user explicitly instructs: always use double precision, no rounding. This approves the full-double standard error-function option presented for production layer segmentation, element preparation and profiles, and resolves the pending D-29 choice for that function.
- Use double arithmetic/storage throughout; do not insert float conversions or compatibility quantization. Ordinary finite IEEE-754 double arithmetic remains inherent. Do not carry over the original helpers' 3e-7 stopping threshold as a requested accuracy target. Preserve the existing lookup grid/interpolation and surrounding physics unless separately changed.
- The original LayerSegmentation.cpp declares float intermediates in gser (line 125) and gcf (line 160), despite double interfaces. erff calls these helpers through gammp, and erffinit stores their results in a double lookup table; storing them as double does not restore lost precision. The original tree remains unchanged.
- This approves implementation, not completed rollout or numerical acceptance. Existing explicit-original-lookup regressions can remain; differences of the production full-double path are to be reviewed with real examples under D-40, not hidden by changing golden tolerances. Earlier notes leaving this specific choice pending are superseded by D-41.

No original Numerical Recipes implementation or float-compatibility step is copied.

- The user approved the proposed correction: “Die müssen auf jedenfall richtig zugeordnet werden.” This resolves the pending D-29 .F assignment decision.
- Source trace: shared/MaterialConstantsHelper.cpp Filldb selects an element with outer i. The anisotropic branch declares a second i for tensor rows and writes film->el[i].F.a[i][j]. Scalar/isotropic assignment uses the outer element index and is not this defect.
